Fig 13:
Mean residual life improvement from UNOS/OPTN data. Stratified by Race, Gender, and Insurance Status with maximum
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}
per strata.

Figure
11
-
13
further reveal the mean residual life improvement stratified by gender (
X_{1}
), race (
X_{2}
), and insurance coverage (
X_{4}
) at different values of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}
. Several inequalities are noteworthy.
First, patients with private insurance performed better than those with public insurance in most of cases, possibly due to socioeconomic status differences and the affordability for disease maintenance and treatment
(
Goldfarb-Rumyantzev
et al. 2006
;
Nicholas, Kalantar-Zadeh and
Norris 2015
)
. Second, the life gains were not very similar across male and female patients, especially the patterns differed between them. Females tended to have life gains positively related to the index; in contrast, males tended to have higher residual life gains at small absolute index. It is likely that heterogeneous kidney disease progression rates and lifestyles might lead to the pattern discrepancy
(
Baylis 2009
;
Okada et al. 2014
;
Pscheidt et al. 2015
)
, though the negligible difference in quantity between genders exemplified the “canceled survival advantage between genders” phenomenon
(
Øien et al. 2006
;
Carrero 2010
;
Cobo et al. 2016
)
.

Survival gains of African Americans with public insurance were not monotonically related to the index regardless of gender given
t
; further, it was observed that African American females experienced greater life expansion compared to their male counterparts. Interestingly, we found the survival gains of African Americans were comparable with those of non-Hispanic Whites, even though these two racial groups had very different mortality among the general population
(
Lewis et al. 2010
)
. However, the African American groups exhibited markedly different indices compared to the non-Hispanic white groups, possibly due to lifestyles and lack of access to healthcare among those groups
(
Kasiske, London and
Ellison 1998
;
Nicholas, Kalantar-Zadeh and
Norris 2013
;
Fedewa et al. 2014
)
.

The Hispanic patients displayed consistent patterns in relation to their insurance type. Notably, there was minimal improvement in life gains when
w
was less than 500 and when
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}>-1.2
. However, at
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}=-1.8
, life gains showed a notable increase as
w
increased. The most significant decrease in life gains was observed under specific conditions: when
t
and
w
were small, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}<-1.5
. These trends held true for both genders and across different insurance types.

Among Asian patients, a distinct pattern emerged in life gains with respect to the variables
t
and
w
. Initially, life gains exhibited a rising trend, followed by a subsequent decline, with fluctuations observed along these dimensions. However, when
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}<0
, the alterations in life gains were less pronounced. Remarkably, the impact of insurance type on survival outcomes varied between genders. For female patients, the choice between private and public insurance did not yield significantly divergent survival gains. Conversely, among male patients, private insurance demonstrated a better outcomes when compared to public insurance.

6
Discussion

Addressing
a severe shortage of organs that are needed to sustain ESRD patients’ life, this work aims to design a feasible strategy to increase
the potential efficiency brought by each available kidney. Instead of
evaluating the patients’ expected survival time, as is done
in the literature, we
consider
the potential residual life prolonged by kidney transplant. By
comparing patients’ expected residual life with and without
transplant, we use their difference to gauge the potential benefit
gained from the transplant; patients with larger
differences may have a higher
priority for organ allocations than those
with smaller values.

A natural extension of our model is to compute the causal effect between two groups. In the absence of a strong confounder “age", it is impossible to draw causal conclusion in this study. However, our comparison between two groups has a capability to analyze the causality as long as all confounders are included. On the other hand, our model compares the transplant cohort to a special case of the nontransplant cohort in which the transplantation would never happen in the future. A more general model will be studied in the future that transplant occurred at any time. Therefore, excepting comparing the mean residual life, many other quantities such as all-cause survivals and hazard ratio will be considered
(
Aalen, Cook and
Røysland 2015
;
Andersen, Syriopoulou and
Parner 2017
;
Syriopoulou, Rutherford and
Lambert 2020
)
.

Our semiparametric regression model of mean
residual life relaxes the parametric assumptions on the
dependence of mean residual life on covariates and
how long a patient has lived.
To strike a balance
between interpretation and flexibility, our procedure enables one to reduce the covariate dimensions from
p
to
d
:
when
d=1
, the model falls to the single index model, while
d=p
corresponds to a completely
nonparametric model. We suggest to use the Validated
Information Criterion
(
Ma and Zhang 2015
)
to choose
d
,
which seems to fare well in practice.

In our model, both the transplant and non-transplant groups are characterized by the same set of indices denoted as
\boldsymbol{\beta}
throughout the study. An alternative would be to conduct separate estimation processes to distinguish the effects of these indices on different transplantation statuses. While our method can accommodate this suggestion by applying the model separately to each group, this separation approach may imply independent progressions for the same patient before and after the transplant. This may be beyond the scope of our primary objective of consistently quantifying survival improvements. To estimate the mean residual life enhancement attributable to inherent factors which reflect patient functional status, we intend to treat each patient’s progression as a cohesive whole. This seems reasonable in the realm of kidney transplantation as studies showed that transplant does not interact significantly with patients’ pre-operative functional status and is “associated with substantial improvement in all stages of functional capacity"
(
Ali et al. 2021
)
.

To ensure estimability, we have assumed the complete follow-up
condition, which is reasonable in clinical studies with high
event rates and long followup
(
Sun, Song and Zhang 2012
)
, such as in
studies with advanced stage cancer patients

(
Chen et al. 2005
)
and ESRD patients

(
Mansourvar, Martinussen and
Scheike 2016
)
. Our data example features
renal failure patients with a long followup, which may satisfy
the assumption. We also acknowledge that, while the complete follow-up condition is a
common assumption
(
Chen et al. 2005
;
Chen and Cheng 2005
;
Tsiatis 1990
;
Sun and Zhang 2009
)
,
it incurs some limitations. For example,

Ying 1993
pointed out that this assumption
implies that the knowledge of the support is obtained in
advance to assure a reasonable maximum followup time
\tau
.

Sun, Song and Zhang 2012
,
Chen and Cheng 2006
and

Mansourvar, Martinussen and
Scheike 2015

proposed various ways of selecting a reasonable
\tau
, all requiring
certain pre-knowledge. Due to these limitations,
in Supplement
4
,
we further relax the complete
followup condition, where we allow an unbounded support for the
event time and only require a tail condition on the
distribution
similar to but weaker than the sub-Gaussian type. Finally, we
are aware that the kidney transplant data
from the U.S. SRTR may represent a biased sample, that is, the included patients were those with access to transplantation. In order to make results generalizable to a more general population, it is vital to take the probability of accessing transplantation into account. Estimation of this probability, however, is challenging because of many tangible and intangible factors involved in the process
(
Axelrod et al. 2008
;
Weng et al. 2010
;
Kucirka, Purnell and
Segev 2015
;
Carrero et al. 2018
)
. More research is warranted.

†
†
funding:
G. Zhao was supported by Faculty Development Program, Portland State University (FEAGXZ). H. Lin was supported by Natural Science Foundation of China (No. 11931014 and 11829101).

Section 1 derives
the estimators for the finite dimensional parameters and nonparametric functions.
Section 2 presents the asymptotic results of these estimators and lists
the sufficient regularity conditions. Section 3 proves the main theorems for
the asymptotic results. Section 4 considers a possible relaxation of an assumption made
on event times.

1
Derivations of the Estimators

1.1
Derivation of an efficient score function

Let
Y(t)=I(Z\geq t)
be the at risk process and

N(t)=I(Z\leq t)\Delta
be the counting process.
Define the filtration
{\cal F}_{t}
to be
\sigma\{N(u),Y(u),{\bf X},I(W\leq u),WI(W\leq u),0\leq u<t\}
, and, thus,

M(t)=N(t)-\int_{0}^{t}Y(s)\lambda\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}ds
is
a martingale with respect to
{\cal F}_{t}
.
The nuisance tangent space, which will be utilized for deriving our
estimator, is obtained as follows.

Proposition 1
.

The nuisance tangent space is

{\cal T}={\cal T}_{1}\oplus{\cal T}_{2}\oplus{\cal T}_{3}
, where each component corresponds to
f_{{\bf X},W}
,
m
defined in (
), and
\lambda_{c}
,
respectively. Specifically,

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[{\bf a}({\bf X},W):E\{{\bf a}({\bf X},W)\}={\bf 0},{\bf a}({\bf X},W)\in{\cal R}^{(p-d)d},\hbox{var}\{{\bf a}({\bf X},W)\}<\infty],

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\times dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}:\forall{\bf h}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}\in{\cal R}^{(p-d)d},

\displaystyle\hbox{var}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}<\infty\bigg),

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf h}(s,{\bf X})dM_{c}(s,{\bf X}):\forall{\bf h}(z,{\bf X})\in{\cal R}^{(p-d)d},\hbox{var}\{{\bf h}(z,{\bf X})\}<\infty\right].

The derivation of Proposition
1
is provided in
Supplement
1.1.1
.
Taking the derivative of the logarithm of (
5
)
with
respect to
\boldsymbol{\beta}
, we obtain
the score function

\displaystyle{\bf S}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\otimes{\bf X}_{l}dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\},

where
m_{1}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot)\}\equiv\partial m\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}/\partial s=\partial m_{T}(s-W,{\bf v})/\partial sI(W\leq s)+\partial m_{N}(s,{\bf v})/\partial s\{1-I(W\leq s)\}
,
{\bf m}_{2}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}\equiv\partial m\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}/\partial{\bf v}=\partial m_{T}(s-W,{\bf v})/\partial{\bf v}I(W\leq s)+\partial m_{N}(s,{\bf v})\allowbreak/\partial{\bf v}\{1-I(W\leq s)\}
,
{\bf m}_{12}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}\equiv\partial{\bf m}_{2}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}/\partial s=\partial^{2}m_{T}(s-W,{\bf v})/\partial s\partial{\bf v}I(W\leq s)+\partial^{2}m_{N}(s,{\bf v})

/\partial s\partial{\bf v}\{1-I(W\leq s)\}
.
We can verify that, at
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}
,
{\bf S}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}\perp{\cal T}_{1}
and

{\bf S}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}\perp{\cal T}_{3}
due to the martingale
properties.
To look for an efficient score by projecting

{\bf S}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}
at
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}
to
{\cal T}_{2}
, we search for

{\bf h}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}
such that

\displaystyle{\bf S}_{\rm eff}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf S}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}-\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{{\bf h}_{1}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\frac{{\bf h}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes{\bf X}_{l}-{\bf h}_{1}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes{\bf X}_{l}-{\bf h}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\times dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\},

where
{\bf h}_{1}^{*}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}=\partial{\bf h}^{*}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}/\partial s
, is orthogonal to
{\cal T}_{2}
.
It implies that, for any
{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},

I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}
, the following must hold

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf a}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}^{\rm T}\left[\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}\right.\right.

(13)

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\frac{{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]ds\right),

where

\displaystyle{\bf a}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}

(14)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle E\left(\left[\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes{\bf X}_{l}-{\bf h}_{1}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes{\bf X}_{l}-{\bf h}^{*}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\times S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\bigg)S\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}

\displaystyle\times\frac{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}},

and
{\bf h}_{1}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}=\partial{\bf h}\{s,{\bf v},\cdot,\cdot\}/\partial s
.
We can choose any
{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}
function. Specifically, by letting
{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}=0
for
s<t
and

{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}={\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
for
s\geq t
with an
arbitrary
function
{\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
, we obtain

\frac{{\bf a}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}+1}-\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{{\bf a}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}ds={\bf 0}.

Solving this integral equation leads to

\displaystyle{\bf a}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}+1\}

\displaystyle\times\exp\left[-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}ds\right]{\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X}),

for function
{\bf c}(\cdot)
. Thus, reusing (
13
), we require that for all

{\bf h}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}[m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}+1]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\exp\left[-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}ds\right]{\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X})^{\rm T}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\left[\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}\right]dt\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E\left[{\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X})^{\rm T}{\bf h}\{0,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq 0),WI(W\leq 0)\}\right].

Letting
{\bf h}\{0,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq 0),WI(W\leq 0)\}={\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
yields the only
possibility of
{\bf c}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X})={\bf 0}
, hence

{\bf a}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}={\bf 0}
.
Inserting the expression of

{\bf a}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}
into (
14
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\bf h}_{1}^{*}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}^{*}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf b}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\},

where

\displaystyle{\bf b}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}=\left\{\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}+1}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}{m\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}}\right\}\otimes\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}}.

Thus an efficient score is

\displaystyle{\bf S}_{\rm eff}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\otimes\left[{\bf X}_{l}-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}}\right]dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}.

1.1.1
Proof of Proposition
1

Proof:
Let
{\cal T}_{1}
,
{\cal T}_{2}
and
{\cal T}_{3}
be the nuisance
tangent
spaces corresponding to
f_{{\bf X},W}
,
m
and
\lambda_{c}

respectively.
The result of
{\cal T}_{1}
follows obviously.
To obtain
{\cal T}_{2}
, let

m\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\rm T}{\bf h}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}
be a sub model of

m\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}
, where
{\bf h}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}\in{\cal R}^{(p-d)d}
with
\hbox{var}[{\bf h}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},

I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}]<\infty,

differentiate the log of (
5
) with
respect to
\boldsymbol{\gamma}
and evaluate it at

\boldsymbol{\gamma}=0
. Then,
{\cal T}_{2}
is

\displaystyle\Delta\left\{\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}}{m_{1}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}}{m\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}}\right\}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{z}\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m^{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1\}{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m^{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta\left\{\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}}{m_{1}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}}{m\{z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq z),WI(W\leq z)\}}\right\}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{z}\left[\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lambda\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[\frac{{\bf h}_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}-\frac{{\bf h}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}.

To obtain
{\cal T}_{3}
,
let

\lambda_{c}(t,{\bf X})\{1+\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\rm T}{\bf h}(t,{\bf X})\}
be
a submodel of
\lambda_{c}(t,{\bf X})
,
where
{\bf h}(z,{\bf X})\in{\cal R}^{(p-d)d},
with
\hbox{var}\{{\bf h}(z,{\bf X})\}<\infty
.
We then obtain
{\cal T}_{3}
as follows

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\hbox{log}f({\bf X},Z,\Delta)}{\partial\boldsymbol{\gamma}}|_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\Delta){\bf h}(Z,{\bf X})-\int_{0}^{Z}{\bf h}(s,{\bf X})\lambda_{c}(s,{\bf X})ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf h}(s,{\bf X})dM_{c}(s,{\bf X}),

where
M_{c}(t,{\bf X})=N_{c}(t)-\int_{0}^{t}I(Z\geq s)\lambda_{c}(s,{\bf X})ds

is a martingale by Theorem 1.3.2 in
Fleming and Harrington 1991
.
Because

\lambda_{c}(t,{\bf X})
can be any positive function,

{\bf h}(s,{\bf X})
can be any function. This leads to the form of
{\cal T}_{3}
.

By taking conditional expectations given
{\bf X}
and
W
,
it follows that
{\cal T}_{1}\perp{\cal T}_{2}
and

{\cal T}_{1}\perp{\cal T}_{3}
.
Further,
{\cal T}_{2}\perp{\cal T}_{3}
because the martingale
integrals associated with
M\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}
and
M_{c}(s,{\bf X})
are
independent
conditional on
{\bf X}
due to
T\;\,\rule[0.0pt]{0.29999pt}{6.69998pt}\hskip-2.5pt\rule[0.0pt]{6.49994pt}{0.29999pt}\hskip-2.5pt\rule[0.0pt]{0.29999pt}{6.69998pt}\;\,C\mid W,{\bf X}
.
∎

1.1.2
Proof of Equations (
6
) and
(
3
)

Proof:
First, we note that, when
t\leq\tau
,

\displaystyle E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}Y(t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}I(T\geq t)I(C\geq t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},{\bf X},W\right\}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}I(T\geq t)I(C\geq t)\mid{\bf X},W\right\}\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left[{\bf X}_{l}S\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\},

where
the second to last equality holds because of
T\;\,\rule[0.0pt]{0.29999pt}{6.69998pt}\hskip-2.5pt\rule[0.0pt]{6.49994pt}{0.29999pt}\hskip-2.5pt\rule[0.0pt]{0.29999pt}{6.69998pt}\;\,C\mid{\bf X},W

and that
\hbox{pr}(T\geq t|{\bf X})
is a function of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}
and
W
.
Similarly, for
t\leq\tau
, we obtain that

E\left\{Y(t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}=S\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}E\left\{S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}.

Hence, when
t\leq\tau
,

\displaystyle\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}Y(t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}}{E\left\{Y(t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}.

(15)

Second, when
t>\tau
,
Y(t)=0
and
S_{c}(t,{\bf X})=0
. Hence, we have a
0/0
scenario in (
15
), in which case,
we define (
15
) to be

\displaystyle\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(\tau,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(\tau,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}=\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}p({\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{p({\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}},

a time-invariant constant.
Here,
p({\bf X})
is defined in Section
3
.
Hence, (
6
) holds over
[0,\infty)
, with the truth of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
being
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}
.

With a generic
\boldsymbol{\beta}
, (
15
) leads to

\displaystyle E\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf g}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes\left[{\bf X}_{l}-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.Y(s)\lambda_{0}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}ds\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf g}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes\left[E\{{\bf X}_{l}Y(s)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}E\{Y(s)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\right]\lambda_{0}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})ds\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf 0},

as the quantity inside the square bracket is zero.
In addition,

\displaystyle E\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf g}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\otimes\left[{\bf X}_{l}-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}\right]dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\right)={\bf 0},

because
dM\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}=dN(s)-Y(s)\lambda_{0}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),W\allowbreak I(W\leq s)\}ds
is a martingale.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle E\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}{\bf g}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}\otimes\left[{\bf X}_{l}-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}\right]dN(s)\right)={\bf 0}.

Hence, (
3
) holds with the truth of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
being
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}
.
∎

1.2
Explicit Expressions of Nonparametric Estimators

1.2.1
Nonparametric Estimators of Hazard and Mean Residual Life Functions and Their Derivatives

The term
W
within the estimator of
\Lambda_{T}
is equivalent to
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}
when conducting the kernel estimation therefore to be omitted thereafter for the sake of ease notation. Thus the estimators of
\Lambda_{T}
and
\Lambda_{N}
have a similar format and only differ in the samples utilized. We propose a general form of the nonparametric estimators of

{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
,

\lambda(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
,
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
, where
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{2}(t,{\bf v})\equiv\partial\Lambda(t,{\bf v})/\partial{\bf v}
and
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{2}(t,{\bf v})\equiv\partial\lambda(t,{\bf v})/\partial{\bf v}
.

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})

(16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{I(Z_{i}\leq t)\Delta_{i}{\bf K}_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i})K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}I(Z_{i}\leq t)\Delta_{i}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i}){\bf K}_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{\{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i})K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\}^{2}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{K_{b}(Z_{i}-t)\Delta_{i}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i})K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})},

(17)

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{K_{b}(Z_{i}-t)\Delta_{i}{\bf K}_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i})K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{b}(Z_{i}-t)\Delta_{i}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i}){\bf K}_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{\{\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(Z_{j}\geq Z_{i})K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\}^{2}},

(18)

The estimators of
{m}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
,
{m}_{T}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
,
m_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}
,
{\bf m}_{2}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},

I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}
, and
{\bf m}_{12}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}
are

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})=e^{{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\int_{t}^{\tau}e^{-{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}ds,

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{T}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})=e^{{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\int_{t}^{\tau}e^{-{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}ds,

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{1}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}dsI(W\leq t)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\{1-I(W\leq t)\}-1,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bf m}_{2}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{T2}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right.

\displaystyle\left.-e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{T2}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right\}I(W\leq t)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{N2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right.

\displaystyle\left.-e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{N2}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right\}\{1-I(W\leq t)\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\bf m}_{12}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{T2}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right.

\displaystyle-\widehat{\lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{T2}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds

\displaystyle\left.+\widehat{\lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{T2}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(s-W,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right\}I(W\leq t)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{N2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right.

\displaystyle-\widehat{\lambda}_{N}\{t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq t),WI(W\leq t)\}e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{N2}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds.

\displaystyle\left.+\widehat{\lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{N2}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})e^{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}\int_{t}^{\infty}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}ds\right\}\{1-I(W\leq t)\}.

2
Asymptotic properties and semiparametric efficiency

We list the regularity conditions for the results of
root-
n
consistency and asymptotic normality of the estimators proposed
in Section
3
.
We also establish semiparametric
efficiency of the
estimator obtained by solving (
3
).

C1

(
kernel function
)
The kernel function
K_{h}(\cdot)=h^{-d}K(\cdot/h)
where

K({\bf a})=\prod_{j=1}^{d}K(a_{j})
for

{\bf a}=(a_{1},...,a_{d})^{\rm T}
is symmetric on each individual
entry and
K(a_{j})
is differentiable,
decreasing when
x\geq 0
, and
\int K(x)dx=1
,

\int x^{j}K(x)dx=0
, for
1\leq j<\nu
,
0<\int x^{\nu}K(x)dx<\infty
, and

\int K^{2}(x)dx
,

\int x^{2}K^{2}(x)dx
,

\int K^{\prime 2}(x)dx
,

\int x^{2}K^{\prime 2}(x)dx
,

\int K^{\prime\prime 2}(x)dx
,

\int x^{2}K^{\prime\prime 2}(x)dx
are all bounded. When there is no confusion,
we use the same
K
for both
univariate and multivariate kernel functions for simplicity.

C2

(
bandwidths
)
The bandwidths
h
and
b
satisfy
h\to 0
,
nh^{2\nu}\to 0
,
b\to 0
and

nh^{d+2}b\to\infty
, where
2\nu>d+1
.

C3

(
density functions of
covariates
)
For all
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in{\cal B}
, the
parameter space,
the probability density function of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}
,

f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
, has a compact support and is
bounded away from zero and
\infty
. Furthermore,
the first and second derivatives of
f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
exist and are Lipschitz continuous.

C4

(
smoothness
)
For all
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in{\cal B}
and
t>0
,

E\{{\bf X}_{j}I(Z_{j}\geq t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}\}

is bounded and its first derivative is a Lipschitz continuous function of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}
.

E\{{\bf X}_{j}{\bf X}_{j}^{\rm T}I(Z_{j}\geq t)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}\}
is a bounded and Lipschitz continuous function of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}
.

C5

(
survival
function
)

For all
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in{\cal B}
and
t\in(0,\tau)
,

E\{S_{c}(t,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}\}
and
f(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
are
bounded away from zero. Their first derivatives with respect
to
t
and first and second derivatives with respect to

\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}
exist and are Lipschitz continuous. In
addition,
S_{c}(\tau,{\bf X})
is bounded
way from zero.

C6

(
boundedness
)
The true parameter
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{0}
is an interior point in
\cal B

and
{\cal B}
is bounded.

C7

(
uniqueness
)
The equation

\displaystyle E\left(\Delta\left[\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}}{m_{1}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}+1}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}}{m\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left.\otimes\left[{\bf X}_{l}-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}Y(Z)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\right\}}{E\left\{Y(Z)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\right\}}\right]\right)={\bf 0}

has a unique solution in
\cal B
.

Conditions
C1
and

C2
are commonly assumed in kernel
regression analysis
(
Silverman 1986
;
Ma and Zhu 2013
)
. Conditions

C3
-
C5
assume boundedness
of event time, censoring time, covariates and their
expectations, which hold for real datasets. The smoothness of several functions is imposed by constraining
their derivatives, which are common conditions
(
Silverman 1978
)
. It is natural to make a boundedness assumption on
the parameter space

\cal B
as in Condition
C6
in practical
problems
(
Härdle
et al. 1997
)
.
Condition
C7
precludes
that the estimating equation
is degenerate.

Theorems
1
and
2
demonstrate
the root-
n
consistency and asymptotical normality of the
profile parameter estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
. The proofs are given in Supplement
3.2
and

3.3
.

Theorem
1
.

Under Conditions

C1
-
C7
,
the estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, obtained by solving (
3
) or
(
3
)
is
consistent,
i.e.

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}\to{\bf 0}
in probability when
n\to\infty
.

Theorem
2
.

Under Conditions

C1
-
C7
,
the estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, obtained by solving (
3
) or
(
3
)
satisfies

\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\to N({\bf 0},{\bf A}^{-1}{\bf B}{{\bf A}^{-1}}^{\rm T})

in distribution when
n\to\infty
, where

\displaystyle{\bf A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{vecl}(\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\rm T}}\mbox{vecl}\left(\Delta{\bf g}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\otimes\left[{\bf a}({\bf X}_{l})-\frac{E\left\{{\bf a}({\bf X}_{l})Y(Z)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\right\}}{E\left\{Y(Z)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\right\}}\right]\right)\right\},

\displaystyle{\bf B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{\mbox{vecl}\left(\Delta{\bf g}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\otimes\left[{\bf a}({\bf X}_{l})-\frac{E\left\{{\bf a}({\bf X}_{l})Y(Z)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\right\}}{E\left\{Y(Z)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\right\}}\right]\right)^{\otimes 2}\right\}.

Here
\mbox{vecl}({\bf A})
represents the vectorization of the lower

(p-d)\times d
block of a generic matrix
{\bf A}
and

{\bf A}^{\otimes 2}={\bf A}{\bf A}^{\rm T}
for any matrix or vector
{\bf A}
.
Note that in (
3
),
{\bf a}({\bf X}_{l})={\bf X}_{l}
and in
(
3
),
{\bf a}({\bf X}_{l})={\bf X}_{l}
,

g\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}=\widehat{\bf m}_{12}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}/\{\widehat{m}_{1}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}+1\}-\widehat{\bf m}_{2}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}/\widehat{m}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}
.
Further, the estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, obtained from solving
(
3
) is semiparametrically efficient and satisfies

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta})\to N\{{\bf 0},(E[{\bf S}_{\rm eff}^{\otimes 2}\{\Delta,Z,{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\})^{-1}\}

in distribution, where
{\bf S}_{\rm eff}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}
is given
in (
1.1
).

With
{\bf S}_{\rm eff}
being a martingale,

\displaystyle E[{\bf S}_{\rm eff}^{\otimes 2}\{Z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq Z),WI(W\leq Z)\}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left[\frac{{\bf m}_{12}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m_{1}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}+1}\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{{\bf m}_{2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}{m\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}}\right]

\displaystyle\left.\left.\otimes\left[{\bf X}_{l}-\frac{E\left\{{\bf X}_{l}S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}{E\left\{S_{c}(s,{\bf X})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}\right\}}\right]\right)^{\otimes 2}dN(s)\right\},

which leads to a consistent estimator of
E[{\bf S}_{\rm eff}^{\otimes 2}\{s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},I(W\leq s),WI(W\leq s)\}]
as follows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i}\left(\frac{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{2}\{z_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\}}{\widehat{\lambda}\{z_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\otimes\left[{\bf x}_{il}-\frac{\widehat{E}\left\{{\bf X}_{l}Y(z_{i})\mid\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\right\}}{\widehat{E}\left\{Y(z_{i})\mid\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\right\}}\right]\right)^{\otimes 2}.

Here
\widehat{E}\left\{Y(z_{i})\mid\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\right\}
,

\widehat{E}\left\{{\bf X}_{l}Y(z_{i})\mid\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\allowbreak,w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\right\}
,

\widehat{\lambda}\{z_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\}

and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}_{2}\{z_{i},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{i},I(w_{i}\leq z_{i}),w_{i}I(w_{i}\leq z_{i})\}

are
given in (
9
), (
10
),
(
17
) and (
18
) respectively.

Theorem
3
.

Under Conditions

C1
-
C7
, the
nonparametric estimators
\widehat{m}_{N}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
and
\widehat{m}_{T}(t-w,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)

satisfy

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh}\left\{\widehat{m}_{N}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})-{m}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})\right\}\to N\{0,\sigma^{2}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})\}

\displaystyle\sqrt{nh}\left\{\widehat{m}_{T}(t-w,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)-{m}_{T}(t-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)\right\}\to N\{0,\sigma^{2}_{T}(t-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)\}

in distribution for all
t
,
w<t
and

{\bf x}
, where

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})=e^{2\Lambda_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\frac{\int K^{2}(u)du}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{\lambda_{N}(r,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}{E\{I(Z\geq r)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}\}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left\{I(r<t)\int_{t}^{\tau}e^{-\Lambda_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}ds+\int_{\max(r,t)}^{\tau}e^{-\Lambda_{N}(s,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}ds\right\}^{2}dr,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{T}(t-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)=e^{2\Lambda_{T}(t-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}\frac{\int K^{2}(u)du}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},W}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{\lambda_{T}(r,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}{E\{I(Z\geq r)\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w\}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left\{I(r<t-w)\int_{t-w}^{\tau}e^{-\Lambda_{T}(s-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}ds+\int_{\max(r,t-w)}^{\tau}e^{-\Lambda_{T}(s-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}ds\right\}^{2}dr.

The proof is provided in
Supplement
3.4
. Theorem

3
implies that
we can even
estimate the variance
\sigma_{T}^{2}(t-w,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)
,
\sigma^{2}_{N}(t,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
, without estimating

\lambda
or
f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x})
, by using

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{T}^{2}(t-w,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I(w\leq t)e^{2\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t-w,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}\int K^{2}(u)du\sum_{i=1}^{n}I(w_{i}\leq t)

\displaystyle\quad\left(\frac{\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t_{(i)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t_{(i-1)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}{1/n\sum_{j=1}^{n}Y_{j}(t_{(i-1)})K_{h}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w_{j}-w)}\right.

\displaystyle\left[I(t_{(i-1)}<t-w)\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(t_{(j)}>t-w)e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t_{(j-1)}-w,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}\{t_{(j)}-\max(t-w,t_{(j-1)})\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\sum_{j=1}^{n}I\{t_{(j)}>\max(t-w,t_{(i-1)})\}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{T}(t_{(j-1)}-w,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x},w)}\{t_{(j)}-\max(t_{(i-1)},t_{(j-1)})\}\right]^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{N}^{2}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{1-I(w\leq t)\}e^{2\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t,\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\int K^{2}(u)du\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{1-I(w_{i}\leq t)\}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t_{(i)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t_{(i-1)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}{1/n\sum_{j=1}^{n}Y_{j}(t_{(i-1)})K_{h}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\right.

\displaystyle\left[I(t_{(i-1)}<t)\sum_{j=1}^{n}I(t_{(j)}>t)e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t_{(j-1)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\{t_{(j)}-\max(t,t_{(j-1)})\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\sum_{j=1}^{n}I\{t_{(j)}>\max(t,t_{(i-1)})\}e^{-\widehat{\Lambda}_{N}(t_{(j-1)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\rm T}{\bf x})}\{t_{(j)}-\max(t_{(i-1)},t_{(j-1)})\}\right]^{2}\right),

where
t_{(1)}<t_{(2)}<\dots<t_{(n_{T})}
are the observed
event times and
t_{(0)}=0
.

Proof: For notation convenience, we prove the results for

. We prove

and skip the remaining results because of the similar arguments.

First, for any
{\bf X}

\boldsymbol{\beta}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})=f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})+O_{p}(n^{-1/2}h^{-1/2}+h^{2}),

To see this, the absolute bias of the left hand
side of
(

\displaystyle\left|E\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right\}-f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int\frac{1}{h}K\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}{h}\right)f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j})d\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j}-f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int K(u)f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}+hu)du-f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int K(u)\left\{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})+f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})hu+O(h^{2})u^{2}\right\}du-f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(h^{2})

under Conditions
C1
-
C3
.
The variance is

\displaystyle\hbox{var}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\hbox{var}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left[EK_{h}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})-\left\{EK_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\left[\int\frac{1}{h^{2}}K^{2}\{(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})/h\}f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j})d\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf x}_{j}-f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})+O(h^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nh}\int K^{2}(u)f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}+hu)du-\frac{1}{n}f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})+O(h^{2}/n)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nh}f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\int K^{2}(u)du+O(h/n)\int u^{2}K^{2}(u)du+\frac{1}{n}|f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})|+O(h^{2}/n).

Therefore, applying the central limit theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K_{h}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{j}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})=f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})+O_{p}(n^{-1/2}h^{-1/2}+h^{2})

\displaystyle E\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{22}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{\partial\left[f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}\right]/\partial\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S^{2}(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}}

\displaystyle\quad\times I(z_{i}\leq z)f(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})dz_{i}+O(n^{-1/2}h^{-3/2}+h^{2}).

In addition

\displaystyle\int\left(I(z_{i}\leq z)\frac{\partial\left[f(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right]/\partial\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}}\right.

\displaystyle-\left.I(z_{i}\leq z)\frac{\partial\left[f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}\right]/\partial\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S^{2}(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}}f(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right)dz_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{2}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}).

Combining
E\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{21}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
and

E\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{22}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})
gives

\displaystyle\left|E\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{2}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})-{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{2}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\right|=O(n^{-1/2}h^{-3/2}+h^{2})

under conditions

-
C5
.

The variance of

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\hbox{var}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{21}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\}+2\hbox{var}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{22}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\}.

The first term

\displaystyle\hbox{var}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{21}(z,\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\hbox{var}\left[\frac{-I(Z_{i}\leq z)\Delta_{i}K_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(Z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(Z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},Z_{i}\}}\right]

\displaystyle+2\hbox{var}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{-I(Z_{i}\leq z)\Delta_{i}K_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(Z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(Z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},Z_{i}\}}O_{p}(A)\right]

The first part is

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\hbox{var}\left[\frac{-I(Z_{i}\leq z)\Delta_{i}K_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(Z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(Z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},Z_{i}\}}\right]

(30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}E\left[\frac{-I(Z_{i}\leq z)\Delta_{i}K_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(Z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(Z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},Z_{i}\}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{n}\left(E\left[\frac{-I(Z_{i}\leq z)\Delta_{i}K_{h}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S(Z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(Z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}_{i}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},Z_{i}\}}\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{nh^{3}}\int\frac{I(z_{i}\leq z)f(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})}{f_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})S^{2}(z_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X})E\{S_{c}(z_{i},{\bf X}_{j})\mid\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\rm T}{\bf X},z_{i}\}}dz_{i}\left\{\int K^{\prime 2}(u)du\right\}

The second part is

\displaystyle\times\left\{\int K^{2}(u)du\right\}

under conditions

.
The second part is
